style="top:-3.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3.05em;"></span><span><span class="mop op-symbol large-op">∑</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.2777em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3117em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mop op-limits"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.05em;"><span style="top:-1.8723em;margin-left:0em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3.05em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:3.05em;"></span><span><span class="mop op-symbol large-op">∑</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.2777em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8641em;"><span style="top:-2.453em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.113em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.247em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:1.6638em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>
<ul>
<li>Zero exactly when the teacher is certain (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> one-hot): hard labels lose nothing there.</li>
<li>Largest for uniform <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">1/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.109em;">N</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li>Across a dataset both methods share the variance from which prefixes were drawn; (a) adds <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">E</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.1138em;vertical-align:-0.2997em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mop op-symbol small-op" style="position:relative;top:0em;">∑</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.162em;"><span style="top:-2.4003em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2997em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-2.4413em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2587em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span> on top. That is the entire gap.</li>
</ul>
<div class="callout" data-callout="note">
<div class="callout-title"><span class="callout-icon" aria-hidden="true"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="18" height="18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M12 20h9"></path><path d="M16.5 3.5a2.12 2.12 0 0 1 3 3L7 19l-4 1 1-4Z"></path></svg></span><span class="callout-title-inner">The advantage of soft targets is data efficiency</span></div>
<div class="callout-content"><p>Same objective (forward KL), same expected gradient, lower variance. With unlimited data both converge to the same student. Soft targets help most where the teacher is uncertain, which is where its distribution carries the most information.</p></div>
</div>
<h4 id="why-hard-labels-still-work">Why hard labels still work<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#why-hard-labels-still-work">#</a></h4>
<p>Each long prefix occurs once, so "50k samples per prefix" would make sequence-level distillation impossible. It is not: the weights are shared, the gradient is averaged over all positions, and what is learned at one prefix transfers to similar ones. Sequence-level distillation from teacher text (Kim and Rush's <em>Sequence-Level Knowledge Distillation</em>) is the most common form in practice.</p>
<h3 id="exposure-bias-and-on-policy-distillation">Exposure bias and on-policy distillation<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#exposure-bias-and-on-policy-distillation">#</a></h3>
<h4 id="compounding-errors">Compounding errors<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#compounding-errors">#</a></h4>
<p>Training prefixes come from the teacher or from data; inference prefixes come from the student. If each token leaves the training distribution with probability <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span></span></span></span> and never returns, a generation of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> tokens survives with probability <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. For <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0.01</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">500</span></span></span></span>: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord">0.9</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord">9</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">500</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8056em;vertical-align:-0.0556em;"></span><span class="mord">0.7%</span></span></span></span>. A student that is 99% reliable per token almost never finishes on-distribution.</p>
<ul>
<li>The argument appears for compositional tasks in Dziri's <em>Faith and Fate</em>; the rigorous imitation-learning version is Ross and Bagnell's.</li>
<li>The assumption "once off, always off" is often false: a model can recover <strong>if it has been trained on what to do after a mistake</strong>.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="on-policy-distillation">On-policy distillation<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#on-policy-distillation">#</a></h4>
<blockquote>
<p>The student writes the training sequences; the teacher supplies its next-token distribution at every position of them.</p>
</blockquote>
<ul>
<li>At position <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6984em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span> after a bad token at <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span>, the teacher's distribution is conditioned on the flawed prefix, so it describes the best continuation from that bad state. The student gets recovery targets for exactly the mistakes it makes.</li>
<li>Training is still one parallel pass: average the per-position cross-entropy over the student-written sequence.</li>
<li>This is DAgger applied to language models. See Agarwal's <em>On-Policy Distillation of Language Models</em> (GKD).</li>
<li>Injected or templated errors are a weaker substitute: you have to guess what the student's mistakes look like.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="mixing-teacher-and-student-prefixes">Mixing teacher and student prefixes<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#mixing-teacher-and-student-prefixes">#</a></h4>
<p>Pure on-policy training from a weak student fails: its text is garbage the teacher has never seen, so the teacher's distributions on it are unreliable.</p>






























<table><thead><tr><th align="left">Method</th><th align="left">Prefix source</th><th align="left">Note</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td align="left"><strong>Supervised KD</strong></td><td align="left">fixed teacher or data text</td><td align="left">train/inference mismatch</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>On-policy (GKD)</strong></td><td align="left">student</td><td align="left">unreliable teacher targets early; sensitive to student initialisation</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Teacher-mixed sampling (MiniLLM)</strong></td><td align="left">blend of both next-token distributions at each step</td><td align="left">reverse-KL objective; mixing stops the student gaming the teacher with degenerate text</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Interleaved sampling (SKD)</strong></td><td align="left">student proposes, teacher replaces tokens outside its top-<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">K</span></span></span></span></td><td align="left">behaves like supervised KD early, like on-policy later, with no hand-tuned schedule</td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="callout" data-callout="tip">
<div class="callout-title"><span class="callout-icon" aria-hidden="true"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="18" height="18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M8.5 14.5A2.5 2.5 0 0 0 11 12c0-1.38-.5-2-1-3-1.07-2.14-.22-4.05 2-6 .5 2.5 2 4.9 4 6.5 2 1.6 3 3.5 3 5.5a7 7 0 1 1-14 0c0-1.15.43-2.29 1-3a2.5 2.5 0 0 0 2.5 2.5Z"></path></svg></span><span class="callout-title-inner">Practical schedule</span></div>
<div class="callout-content"><p>Start from a pretrained student, begin with mostly teacher-written prefixes and shift to student-written ones. The acceptance rate of student tokens under the teacher is a signal that sets the mix automatically.</p></div>
</div>
<h3 id="black-box-distillation">Black-box distillation<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#black-box-distillation">#</a></h3>
<h4 id="what-each-level-of-access-allows">What each level of access allows<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#what-each-level-of-access-allows">#</a></h4>






























<table><thead><tr><th align="left">Teacher exposes</th><th align="left">Available</th><th align="left">Ruled out</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td align="left"><strong>Text only</strong></td><td align="left">sequence-level (forward KL, hard labels)</td><td align="left">soft targets, reverse KL, on-policy scoring, internals</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Text + reasoning</strong></td><td align="left">sequence-level on rationale + answer</td><td align="left">same as above</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Log-probabilities</strong></td><td align="left">token-level soft targets, on-policy, reverse KL</td><td align="left">internals</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Weights</strong></td><td align="left">everything, including hidden states and attention</td><td align="left">nothing</td></tr></tbody></table>
<ul>
<li>Hiding reasoning text blocks sequence-level distillation <em>of the reasoning</em>. Withholding log-probabilities blocks token-level and on-policy methods. They are different protections.</li>
<li>Visible reasoning does <strong>not</strong> enable on-policy distillation: the teacher wrote that text.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="rationales">Rationales<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#rationales">#</a></h4>
<p>Training on reasoning followed by answer supervises many more tokens per example and makes the answer easier to predict from what precedes it. Hsieh's <em>Distilling Step-by-Step</em> shows small models need far fewer examples when rationales are an extra target. The cost: outputs are much longer, so by <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ε</span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6644em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> a small student has more chances to derail before the answer.</p>
<h4 id="protecting-against-distillation">Protecting against distillation<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#protecting-against-distillation">#</a></h4>
<ul>
<li>Providers hide raw chains of thought partly for this reason (competitive advantage is on OpenAI's own list of reasons).</li>
<li>Savani's <em>Antidistillation Sampling</em> keeps traces visible but perturbs sampling to poison them: in their MMLU example a 72% teacher yields a 52% student from ordinary traces and about 40% from poisoned ones.</li>
<li>Hiding is not airtight; 2026 reports describe raw traces recovered through provider APIs.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="building-a-dataset-without-prompts">Building a dataset without prompts<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#building-a-dataset-without-prompts">#</a></h4>
<ul>
<li>Write a few seed prompts, have the teacher generate more, then have it answer them.</li>
<li>Generated prompts are <strong>narrower</strong> than real ones: every call samples from the same high-probability region.</li>
<li>Counters: higher temperature, a different random subset of seeds per call, conditioning on a sampled topic or style, removing near-duplicates. Detection: compare token statistics with a sample of real prompts.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="one-sample-or-many-per-prompt">One sample or many per prompt?<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#one-sample-or-many-per-prompt">#</a></h4>
<ul>
<li>Default: more distinct prompts. Ten answers to one prompt overlap; ten prompts cover ten contexts that shared weights can pool.</li>
<li>Extra samples are informative only where the teacher's answers vary (<span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0728em;vertical-align:-0.2587em;"></span><span class="mop op-symbol small-op" style="position:relative;top:0em;">∑</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-2.4413em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">i</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2587em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> large): open-ended tasks, several valid solutions, genuine uncertainty.</li>
<li>Agreement among samples is an empirical confidence estimate without log-probabilities; use it to filter or down-weight.</li>
</ul>
<h3 id="intermediate-representations">Intermediate representations<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#intermediate-representations">#</a></h3>
<h4 id="hidden-state-matching">Hidden-state matching<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#hidden-state-matching">#</a></h4>
<p>Student state <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0883em;vertical-align:-0.247em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-2.453em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.247em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8491em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span style="top:-2.3567em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.0714em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size3 size1 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1433em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, teacher state <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0883em;vertical-align:-0.247em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord <ul>
<li><strong>Project the student up, not the teacher down.</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0913em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">∥</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0913em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">∥</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span></span></span></span> and the student both free has the trivial solution <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0</span></span></span></span>. Projecting up has a fixed, input-dependent target, so one constant vector cannot fit it.</li>
<li>Used as an auxiliary loss next to output distillation, not alone.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="what-the-loss-constrains">What the loss constrains<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#what-the-loss-constrains">#</a></h4>
<ul>
<li><strong>Basis-free.</strong> If student B has states <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2806em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> for an invertible <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span></span></span></span>, it reaches the same loss with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0502em;">B</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9641em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">A</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0077em;">R</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">−</span><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. No individual neuron means anything to the loss.</li>
<li><strong>Linear recoverability.</strong> The loss is small iff the teacher's state can be reconstructed from the student's by a linear map. Information present only in a nonlinear encoding counts as lost.</li>
<li><strong>PCA floor.</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8413em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">h</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> varies in at most <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> dimensions. With (centred) teacher variances <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">λ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≥</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7719em;vertical-align:-0.136em;"></span><span class="minner">⋯</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≥</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9447em;vertical-align:-0.2503em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">λ</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span style="top:-2.3567em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.0714em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size3 size1 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1433em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2503em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> along principal directions, the best case captures the top <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" below <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.15em;vertical-align:-0.4em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mop class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3448em;"><span style="top:-2.3567em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.0714em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.5em;"></span><span class="sizing </ul>
<div class="callout" data-callout="note">
<div class="callout-title"><span class="callout-icon" aria-hidden="true"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="18" height="18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M12 20h9"></path><path d="M16.5 3.5a2.12 2.12 0 0 1 3 3L7 19l-4 1 1-4Z"></path></svg></span><span class="callout-title-inner">Weakness of hidden-state matching</span></div>
<div class="callout-content"><p>It rewards reproducing the teacher's highest-variance directions, which need not be the ones that matter for predicting the next token.</p></div>
</div>

<ul>
<li><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.9694em;vertical-align:-0.2861em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">Q</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.2861em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.1389em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">K</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are weights and depend on the width. The <strong>map</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span></span></span></span> is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">×</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> whatever <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8444em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">d</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3361em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> are; row <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span> is a distribution over positions.</li>
<li>So no projection is needed: compare maps directly, by row-wise cross-entropy <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.2771em;vertical-align:-0.4358em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mop op-symbol small-op" style="position:relative;top:0em;">∑</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.162em;"><span style="top:-2.4003em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">j</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.4358em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-2.4413em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span><span class="mpunct mtight">,</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">j</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3948em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">lo<span style="margin-right:0.0139em;">g</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8413em;"><span style="top:-2.4413em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">t</span><span class="mpunct mtight">,</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0572em;">j</span></span></span></span><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0576em;">S</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3948em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> or by squared error.</li>
<li>Remaining mismatch: head count. Heads have no natural order, so pairing head <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span> with head <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span> is arbitrary. MiniLMv2 concatenates queries and keys across heads and re-splits them into the same number of groups on both sides.</li>
</ul>

<h3 id="practical-issues">Practical issues<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#practical-issues">#</a></h3>
<h4 id="the-capacity-gap">The capacity gap<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#the-capacity-gap">#</a></h4>
<p>A much stronger teacher can give a worse student. Its distributions depend on distinctions the student cannot represent; under forward KL the student averages over them and smears mass onto tokens that are wrong in each specific context, and those positions seed compounding errors. A weaker teacher's distributions can be fitted without smearing.</p>
<p>Remedies:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Reverse KL on student-written text</strong>: the student commits to the modes it can represent.</li>
<li><strong>Simplify the target</strong>: filter or truncate the teacher's distribution.</li>
<li><strong>Teacher assistant</strong> (Mirzadeh's <em>Improved Knowledge Distillation via Teacher Assistant</em>): distil 70B into 7B, then 7B into 1B. Each gap is smaller, but the copy errors chain.</li>
</ul>
<p>Toy model of the trade-off (invented numbers): a step across ratio <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">r</span></span></span></span> keeps <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1076em;">f</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0278em;">r</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> of performance; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span> equal steps keep <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.138em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1076em;">f</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">7</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord">0</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.888em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1/</span><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose"><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>.</p>



































<table><thead><tr><th align="left"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0315em;">k</span></span></span></span></th><th align="left">ratio per step</th><th align="left"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1076em;">f</span></span></span></span></th><th align="left">kept overall</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td align="left">1</td><td align="left">70</td><td align="left">0.80</td><td align="left">0.80</td></tr><tr><td align="left">2</td><td align="left">8.4</td><td align="left">0.93</td><td align="left">0.86</td></tr><tr><td align="left">4</td><td align="left">2.9</td><td align="left">0.97</td><td align="left"><strong>0.89</strong></td></tr><tr><td align="left">8</td><td align="left">1.7</td><td align="left">0.98</td><td align="left">0.85</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Interior optimum: a few intermediate models. Each one also costs a full distillation run.</p>
<h4 id="a-two-teacher-variant">A two-teacher variant<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#a-two-teacher-variant">#</a></h4>
<p>Idea from the session: train the 1B student on 70B-written text with soft targets from a 7B model that was not itself distilled from the 70B one. This is the mixed objective with two teachers: hard labels from the 70B model (unbiased, noisy, the only route for its knowledge) and soft targets from the 7B model (low variance, biased towards what the 7B model already believes). Where the teachers disagree the two terms conflict, and those are the positions where the 70B model knows more. Open question whether it beats the chain; not checked against the multi-teacher literature.</p>
<h4 id="tokenizer-mismatch">Tokenizer mismatch<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#tokenizer-mismatch">#</a></h4>
<blockquote>
<p>Anything that passes through plain text survives; anything defined per token or per position breaks.</p>
</blockquote>








































<table><thead><tr><th align="left">Method</th><th align="left">Different tokenizers?</th><th align="left">Why</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td align="left"><strong>Token-level soft targets</strong></td><td align="left">breaks</td><td align="left">distributions over different vocabularies</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Sequence-level on teacher text</strong></td><td align="left">works</td><td align="left">re-tokenise the teacher's string with the student's tokenizer</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>On-policy, per-token scoring</strong></td><td align="left">breaks</td><td align="left">positions and vocabularies do not line up</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>On-policy, whole-string <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop">lo<span style="margin-right:0.0139em;">g</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0359em;">y</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span></strong></td><td align="left">works</td><td align="left">the teacher scores the string with its own tokenizer; one sparse number per sequence</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Hidden states</strong></td><td align="left">breaks</td><td align="left">position <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6151em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">t</span></span></span></span> covers different text; needs alignment (e.g. pooling over words)</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Attention maps</strong></td><td align="left">breaks</td><td align="left"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6667em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">×</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> with different <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Dense signal without matching vocabularies: Minixhofer's <em>Universal Cross-Tokenizer Distillation via Approximate Likelihood Matching</em> compares the likelihood of chunks of text that both tokenizations delimit identically. See <a href="/notes/tokenization/">Tokenization</a>.</p>
<h3 id="why-distillation-works">Why distillation works<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#why-distillation-works">#</a></h3>
<h4 id="targets-as-estimators-of-the-true-distribution">Targets as estimators of the true distribution<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#targets-as-estimators-of-the-true-distribution">#</a></h4>
<p>Let <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8831em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> be the true next-token distribution. style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>, squared norm 0.0005</td><td align="left"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">0.81</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">×</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0.62</span></span></span></span></td><td align="left">0.50</td></tr><tr><td align="left"><strong>Teacher</strong> <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">0.6</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">0.25</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">0.15</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span></td><td align="left"><span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">E</span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8831em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></td><td align="left">small</td><td align="left">0.015</td></tr></tbody></table>
<ul>
<li>The teacher saw the same single token at this prefix. Its low variance comes from <strong>pooling many similar contexts</strong> through shared weights; averaging <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span></span></span></span> samples divides variance by <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">m</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li>The price is bias, from treating similar contexts alike and from the teacher's own errors. It is visible as the teacher's excess loss on held-out text: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0813em;">H</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.0715em;">K</span><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.6887em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">∗</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∥</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3283em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li>Label smoothing also reduces variance, but with a context-independent vector: it adds no information. The gain of distillation is context-specific information, not smoothing. See <a href="/notes/bias-variance-trade-off/">Bias Variance Trade-off</a>, <a href="/notes/entropy/">Entropy</a>.</li>
</ul>
<p>Three reasons, then: information about unsampled tokens, lower-variance targets, and both bought with a bias.</p>
<h3 id="connections">Connections<a class="anchor" aria-hidden="true" hidden href="#connections">#</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong><a href="/notes/variational-inference/">Variational Inference</a></strong>: same two divergences, opposite one is tractable, because there you cannot sample from <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>.</li>
<li><strong>Imitation learning</strong>: exposure bias is behaviour cloning's compounding error; on-policy distillation is DAgger. See <a href="/notes/inverse-reinforcement-learning/">Inverse Reinforcement Learning</a>.</li>
<li><strong>Quantal Response Equilibrium</strong>: the temperature softmax is the logit response function; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">T</span></span></span></span> plays the role of the inverse rationality parameter.</li>
<li><strong><a href="/notes/ensemble-methods/">Ensemble Methods</a></strong>: the original motivation was compressing an ensemble into one network.</li>
<li><strong><a href="/notes/low-rank-adaptation/">Low-Rank Adaptation</a></strong>: both are rank arguments. LoRA limits the rank of a weight update; the projection <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.1389em;">W</span></span></span></span> limits the rank of the reconstruction of the teacher's activations.</li>
<li><strong>LLM-to-LLM alignment in social simulation</strong>: fitting one model's action distribution to another's is distillation of a policy. The divergence decides whether mixed strategies survive (forward) or collapse to a pure one (reverse).</li>
</ul>

align="left">Forward is mean-seeking; reverse is mode-seeking</td></tr><tr><td align="left">"Soft targets minimise reverse KL"</td><td align="left">The sum is weighted by <span class="katex"><span forward KL plus <span class="katex"><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" hard to compute"</td><td align="left">True in VI only.
